<header>VII. Of Six Points, Part 4.</header>

## IV.
# How Christ Shall Deliver the Kingdom to His Father.

### Summaries.

In the creation, God moved in the <term>Center of Nature</term> <note>In the theology of Jacob Böhme, the "Center of Nature" refers to the foundational, energetic forces that give rise to all existence, often characterized by tension and longing.</note>: in the Incarnation, He moved according to His heart. § 1. There the Son held the governance, and the Holy Spirit drew humanity into the light of love. 2. At the end, however, both properties of the Father and the Son become active at once, 3. in the <term>Economy</term> <note>original: *Oeconomie*; here meaning the divine administration or "household management" of the universe and salvation history.</note> of the Holy Spirit. 4. 5.

1. In the creation of the world and of all being, the Father moved according to His property, as with the Center of Nature, with the <term>Dark- and Fire-World</term> <note>These represent the first two of Böhme's "three worlds": the dark world of potentiality and the fiery world of energy and judgment.</note>; this remained in movement and in governance until the Father moved according to His heart (and the Light-World), and God became man: There, the love of the light overcame the Father's <term>fierce property</term> <note>original: *grimme Eigenschaft*; refers to the "wrath" or harsh, rigorous quality of divine fire before it is tempered by love.</note>, and the Father governed in the Son with love.

2. There, the Son held the governance in those who clung to God: and the Holy Spirit (who proceeds from the Father and Son) drew humanity in the light of love, through the Son, to God the Father.

3. But at the end, the Holy Spirit moves in the Father’s property, and also in the Son’s property, and both properties become active at once; and the Spirit of the Father opens Himself in fire and light, and also in the <term>fierceness</term> <note>original: *Grimm*.</note> of the dark world; there, the governance falls back to the Father. For the Holy Spirit shall govern eternally, and be an eternal "Opener" in the Light-World and also in the Dark-World.

4. For the two worlds will stand still, and the Holy Spirit, who proceeds from the Father and the Son, leads the governance eternally in the two worlds, according to the quality and property of each world.

5. He alone will be the Opener of wonders; and thus the eternal governance—which He conducts with the Spirit—belongs to the Father (who is All).

<meta>catchword: V. Of</meta>

<summary>This section explains the cosmic transition of divine rule from the Father (Creation/Law) to the Son (Incarnation/Love) and finally to the Holy Spirit, who reconciles and governs both the light and dark realms at the end of time.</summary>
<keywords>Creation, Incarnation, Holy Spirit, Center of Nature, Light-World, Dark-World, Governance, Fierceness, Divine Economy</keywords>

--- Page 109 ---
<meta>This page transitions from discussing the governance of the Holy Spirit and the transformation of the "fierce property" into love, to a deep metaphysical definition of "Magic" (Magia). In Jacob Böhme’s thought, Magic is not sorcery but the fundamental creative power of the Divine Will that brings spirit into tangible substance.</meta>

<header>Point 5. Of Mystical Points.</header>

# V.

## On Magic; What Magic Is? What the Magical Ground Is?

### Summaries.

Magic is the mother of eternity, the being of all beings; §. 1. it is only a will, and the primal state of nature. 2. 3. It is not a substance, but the desiring spirit of the substance, 4. and the substance is its body. 5. It is the mystery of the Trinity <note>original: *Drey-Zahl*, literally "three-number"</note>, 6. and the desire in God, 7. 8. and possesses within itself the <term>FIAT</term> <note>Latin for "let it be done," referring to the divine creative command in Genesis.</note>. 9. The imagination is soft; the desire is rough. 10. Magic is a mother in all three worlds, 11. it brings into being what the will models, 12. and makes the divine flesh, spirit, understanding, and senses. 13-16. The understanding guides Magic, 17. for it is the power-fire. 18. Magic is good in the good; but in the evil, it is <term>Necromancy</term> <note>original: *Nigromantia*; Böhme uses this to refer to the misuse of spiritual power or "black magic."</note>. 19. It is the mother of Philosophy, 20. but Philosophy is the understanding and leads Magic. 21. It is the book of all scholars, 22. and the best Theology. 23. Thus, he who slanders it is a fool. ibid. 24.

1. Magic is the mother of eternity, the being of all beings, for it makes itself; and is understood in the desire.

2. In itself, it is nothing but a will; and this same will is the great mystery of all wonders and secrets, and yet leads itself into being through the imagination of the hungry desire.

3. It is the <term>primal state</term> <note>original: *Urstand*; the foundational condition or origin of existence.</note> of nature; its desire creates an imagination, and the imagination is only the will of the desire. But the desire creates in the will such a being as the will is in itself.

4. True Magic is not a substance, but the desiring spirit of the substance: It is an <term>unsubstantial matrix</term> <note>original: *unsubstantialishe Matrix*; a non-material womb or source from which things are birthed into reality.</note>, and yet reveals itself in the substance.

5. Magic is spirit, and substance is its body, and yet both are only one, just as body and soul are only one person.

6. Magic is the greatest secret, for it is above nature; it makes nature according to the form of its will: It is the mystery of the Trinity <note>original: *Drey-Zahl*</note>, understanding the will in the desire for the heart of God.

7. It is the forming in the Divine Wisdom, as a desire in the Trinity, in which the eternal wonder of the Tri-

<meta>catchword: Tri-</meta>

<summary>This page defines Magic (Magia) not as occultism, but as the fundamental creative force of the Divine Will that acts as a "mother" to all existence. Böhme describes it as the spiritual mechanism that bridges the gap between God's will and the physical or spiritual substance of the universe.</summary>
<keywords>Magic, Magia, Divine Will, Trinity, FIAT, Imagination, Urstand, Matrix, Philosophy, Theology</keywords>

--- Page 110 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of Magic (Magia) as the fundamental creative force in Jacob Böhme’s cosmology. While the previous page established Magic as a "mother" and a "will," this page explores its relationship to the Trinity, the divine command, and the formation of physical and spiritual substance.</meta>

VII. Of Six Points 5.

7. The <term>Trinity</term> <note>original: *Drey-Zahl*, literally "three-number," referring to the Triune God.</note> desires to reveal itself along with Nature: Thus, it is the desire that introduces itself into the dark nature, and through nature to the fire, and through the fire—by way of dying or wrath—into the light of the Majesty.

8. It is not Majesty itself, but rather the desire within the Majesty. It is the desire of the Divine Power, not the power itself, but the hunger or the longing within the power; it is not omnipotence, but the guide within the power and might. The Heart of God is the power, and the Holy Spirit is the opening of that power.

9. It is, however, the desire within the power, and also within the guiding Spirit; for it possesses within itself the **FIAT** <note>Latin for "let it be done," the creative word of God.</note>: whatever the will-spirit opens within it, it leads that into a <term>being</term> <note>original: *Wesen*; in this context, it refers to both essence and tangible substance.</note> through the <term>astringency</term> <note>original: *Herbickeit*, a key term in Böhme’s thought meaning a harsh, pulling, or contracting quality.</note> which is the **FIAT**, all according to the model of the will. As the will models it in wisdom, so the desiring Magic takes it in; for it has imagination as a delight within its property.

10. The imagination is gentle and soft, resembling water; but the desire is rough and dry, like a hunger. It makes the soft thing hard, and is found in all things, for it is the greatest being in the Godhead: it leads the <term>abyss</term> <note>original: *Abgrund*, the bottomless void.</note> into a ground, and the "nothing" into "something."

11. In Magic lie all the forms of the being of all beings; she is a mother in all three worlds, and makes every thing according to the model of its will. She is not the understanding itself, but she is a maker according to the understanding, and allows herself to be used for either good or evil.

12. Everything that the will models in wisdom—provided the will of the understanding also enters into it—Magic makes that into a substantial being. She serves those who love God within God’s being, for she creates divine being within the understanding, and takes that from the imagination, as if from the gentleness of the light.

13. It is she who makes divine flesh; and the understanding is derived from wisdom, for it is a discerner of colors, powers, and virtue. The understanding leads the right, true spirit with a bridle; for the spirit is flying, and the understanding is its fire.

14. The spirit is not departing in such a way that it would deviate from the understanding; rather, it is the will of the understanding. However, the senses within the understanding are those which fly out and deviate.

<meta>catchword: 15. For</meta>

<summary>This page defines Magic as the active, desiring force that gives form to the divine will, transforming the "nothing" of the abyss into the "something" of created existence. It explains how Magic acts as a "mother" that manifests the designs of the understanding into physical or spiritual reality, specifically through the power of the FIAT.</summary>
<keywords>Magic, Magia, Trinity, Majesty, FIAT, Astringency, Herbickeit, Being, Wesen, Abyss, Abgrund, Imagination, Wisdom</keywords>

--- Page 111 ---
<meta>This page continues the exploration of Magic (Magia) from the previous page, shifting focus to how the human senses and the intellect (Verstand) interact with this primal creative force. It defines the hierarchical relationship between Magic, Nature, and Philosophy.</meta>

# Point 5. Mystical Points. 95

15. For the senses are the flash from the fire-spirit, and in the light, they carry within themselves the flames of Majesty; and in the darkness, they carry the flash of terror, as a fierce flash from the fire.

16. The senses are such a subtle spirit that they enter into all <term>beings</term> <note>original: *Wesen*; meaning both essence and physical substance.</note>, and they draw into themselves from all beings. But the understanding tests everything in its fire; it rejects the evil and retains the good, whereupon Magic—its mother—takes it and brings it into a being.

17. Magic is the mother to nature, and the understanding is the mother out of nature: Magic leads into a fierce fire, and the understanding leads its own mother, Magic, out of the fierce fire into its own fire.

18. For the understanding is the power-fire, and Magic is the burning fire; and yet it is not to be understood as literal fire, but rather the power or mother to the fire: the fire is called the <term>principle</term> <note>original: *Principium*.</note>, and Magic is called <term>desire</term> <note>original: *Begierde*.</note>.

19. Through Magic everything is accomplished, both good and evil; its own specific effect is <term>necromancy</term> <note>original: *Nigromantia*; Böhme uses this term to describe the dark or self-willed application of magical power.</note>, and yet it divides itself into all properties: In the good, it (Magic) is good, and in the evil, it is evil. It serves the children of **God’s** kingdom, and the sorcerers in the devil’s kingdom: For the understanding can make of it whatever it will; it is without understanding, and yet it grasps all things, for it is the comprehension of all things.

20. One cannot express its depth, for from eternity it is a foundation and a holder of all things; it is a master of philosophy, and also a mother of the same.

21. But philosophy leads Magic, its mother, according to its pleasure. Just as the Divine power—as the Word (or the Heart of **God**) leads the stern Father into gentleness; so also does philosophy [as the understanding] lead its mother into a gentle <term>divine quality</term> <note>original: *Göttliche Qual*. Böhme uses the word *Qual* to signify both a "source-welling" or "quality" and a "torment" or "laboring" of the spirit.</note>.

22. Magic is the book of all students: everything that wishes to learn must first learn in Magic, whether it be a high or low art; even the farmer in the field must go to the school of Magic if he wishes to till his field.

23. Magic is the best <term>theology</term> <note>original: *Theologia*.</note>; for in it, true faith is grounded and found: and he is a fool who scolds it, for he does not know it; he slanders **God** and himself, and is more a juggler than a sensible <term>theologian</term> <note>original: *Theologus*.</note>.

<meta>catchword: 24. Just as</meta>

<summary>This page defines Magic as a neutral, foundational desire-force that is directed by the "Understanding." It argues that Magic is the basis for all practical and spiritual disciplines, from farming to theology.</summary>
<keywords>Senses, Fire-spirit, Magic, Understanding, Nature, Desire, Necromancy, Philosophy, Theology, Divine Quality</keywords>

--- Page 112 ---
<meta>This page concludes the discussion on the sixth point regarding the nature of Magic and transitions into a formal definition of the "Mystery" (Mysterium). It continues the theme of Magic as a creative, internal force rather than an external art, emphasizing that true understanding comes from within the "mirror of wisdom" rather than through intellectual study alone.</meta>

# VII. Regarding Point 6. 96

24. Just like someone who stands before a mirror and does not know what the conflict is, because he sees only from the outside; so too does the unjust <term>theologian</term> <note>original: *Theologus*.</note> look at <term>Magic</term> <note>original: *Magia*.</note> through a looking-glass and understands nothing of its power. For it is Divine, and he is un-Divine—though it can also be devilish, according to the property of each <term>principle</term> <note>original: *Principii*.</note>. In short: Magic is the acting in the will-spirit.

## VI.
### On the Mystery; what it may be?

->Summaries<-

The Mystery is the magical will. §. 1. The <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: *Mysterium Magnum*.</note> is the hiddenness of the Godhead; understand this according to the property of the mirror. 2. 3. In this same Mystery, the Magus has the power to do what he wills, 4. when he is armed within that same being. 5.

1. The Mystery is nothing other than the magical will, which is still caught in desire; it may form itself in the mirror of wisdom however it wills: and as it forms itself in the <term>tincture</term> <note>original: *Tinctur*; in Böhme’s thought, the spiritual essence or "dye" that transforms and gives quality to matter.</note>, it is thus grasped in Magic and brought into a <term>being</term> <note>original: *Wesen*; meaning a concrete essence or substance.</note>.

2. For the Great Mystery is nothing other than the hiddenness of the <term>Godhead</term> <note>original: *Gottheit*.</note>, along with the essence of all essences; from it, one Mystery proceeds after another; and each Mystery is the mirror and prototype of the other. It is the great wonder of eternity, in which everything is enclosed and has been seen from eternity in the mirror of wisdom; nothing happens that was not known from eternity in the mirror of wisdom.

3. But you must understand this according to the properties of the mirror—according to all the forms of nature, such as light and darkness; according to tangibility and intangibility; according to love and wrath, such as fire and light (as has been mentioned in other places).

4. In this same Mystery, the Magus has the power to act according to his will and can make what he wills.

5. But he must be armed within that same being in which he wishes to act, or he will be cast out as a stranger and given over into the power of its spirits, to deal with him according to their desire. Nothing more is to be mentioned here concerning this, because of the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: *Turba*; Böhme’s term for the "Disturber" or the spirit of wrath and chaos that breaks things apart when the divine order is violated.</note>.

->T H E   E N D.<-

<summary>This page defines the "Great Mystery" as the hidden, eternal essence of God and the source of all reality, which acts like a mirror reflecting all possibilities. It warns that a practitioner (Magus) must be spiritually "armed" or aligned with this essence to avoid being overwhelmed by the Turba, the force of divine wrath or chaos.</summary>
<keywords>Mysterium Magnum, Great Mystery, Magic, Magus, Godhead, Mirror of Wisdom, Tincture, Turba, Divine Will</keywords>

<summary>This page introduces a mystical exploration of the "Mysterium," focusing on how the Eternal One and the "Primal Source" emerge from Nothingness through the force of "Desire" or "Longing." The text is a fragmentary introduction to a cosmogony explaining the origin of Nature and spiritual existence.</summary>

--- Page 117 ---
<meta>This page begins Jacob Böhme's "Mysterium Pansophicum," which continues his exploration of the "Ungrund" and the emergence of the will from the void, concepts previously introduced in his earlier works on the nature of God and creation.</meta>

<page-num>97</page-num>
<header>VIII.</header>

# PANSOPHIC MYSTERY.
<note>The term "Pansophic" refers to "all-wisdom," a movement in the 17th century seeking to synthesize all knowledge—scientific, mystical, and theological—into a single system.</note>
->Or<-
### Thorough Report
# On the Earthly and Hea-
# venly Mystery.

### The First Text.
->Summaries.<-
The eternal understanding of Magic creates within itself where nothing is. §.
<note>Magic (German: *Magiä*): For Böhme, magic is not sorcery but the divine "desire" or spiritual power that manifests something out of the void.</note>

The Groundless <note>Groundless (German: *Ungrund*): Böhme’s famous term for the absolute void or "nothingness" that precedes the existence of God and the universe.</note> is an eternal Nothing, yet it makes an eternal beginning as a longing; <note>Longing (German: *Sucht*): An intense, driving craving or hunger for being.</note> for the Nothing is a longing for Something. And since there is nothing to give the Something, the longing itself is the giving of that which is also a nothing, as merely a desiring longing. And that is the eternal origin of Magic, which creates within itself where nothing is; it makes Something out of Nothing, and that only within itself, and since that same longing is also a nothing, as merely a will: it has Nothing, and there is also nothing to give it something, and it has no place to find or lay itself down.

### The Second Text.
->Summaries.<-
In the Nothing is a longing, which makes within itself the will for Something. §. 1. The will, however, is a spirit and a Magus, and is caused by the longing; 2. from which Nature, and the spirit of Nature, are to be conceived. 3.

Since then there is a longing in the Nothing, it creates for itself the will for Something: and that same will is a spirit, like a thought, which proceeds from the longing, and is the seeker of the longing, for it finds its mother as the longing. Now that same will is a Magus in its mother: for it has found Something in the Nothing, namely its mother, and so when it has found its mother, it now has a place for its dwelling.

<meta>catchword: 2. And</meta>

---

<page-type>diagram</page-type>
<page-num>8</page-num>
<header>8.</header>

# Earthly and Heavenly Mystery

<image-desc>
A complex theosophical engraving framed by a double-line border. At the top, a crown rests above a chalice-like figure. Centered in the upper half is a radiant Eye of Providence within a triangle, superimposed on a large hexagram. Surrounding the hexagram is a circular halo of twenty-four smaller eyes, each set within a flame. 

In the center of the diagram, a rectangular tablet is held up by a cross. The tablet contains the INRI inscription (Jesus of Nazareth, King of the Jews) in three languages:
1. Hebrew: <note>original: ישוע הנצרי / מלך היהודים</note> Jesus the Nazarene / King of the Jews.
2. Greek: <note>original: Ιησους ο ναζωραιος / ο βασιλευς των / ιουдαιων</note> Jesus the Nazarene / the King of / the Jews.
3. Latin: <note>original: Iesus Nazarenus Rex ille Iudeorum</note> Jesus the Nazarene, King of the Jews.

At the bottom of the diagram, a circular medallion contains a large letter "I" <note>likely representing the "Ich" (I) or the unity of the Divine Name</note>. To the left of the medallion lies a bundle of birch rods (a broom or scourge), and to the right is a sword. 
</image-desc>

<summary>This page introduces Böhme's cosmogony, explaining how the "Groundless" void generates a craving (longing) that becomes a "will," which acts as a magical force to create existence from nothing. The accompanying diagram illustrates the divine presence through symbols like the Eye of Providence, the Trinity, and the kingship of Christ.</summary>
<keywords>Groundless, Ungrund, Magic, Will, Longing, Nothingness, Cosmogony, Jacob Böhme, Eye of Providence, INRI</keywords>

--- Page 118 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Second Text" of the "Pansophic Mystery" and begins the "Third Text," further defining the relationship between the eternal Will and the driving "Longing" or "Craving" that constitutes the foundation of all existence.</meta>

<page-num>98</page-num>
<header>VIII. Of the Earthly Text 3.</header>

2. And understand herein how the Will is a spirit, and something other than the craving desire. For the Will is an unfeeling and unrecognizable life; but the craving is found by the Will, and is an essence within the Will. Now it is recognized that the craving is a <term>Magic</term><note>original: *Magia*. For Böhme, "Magic" is the spiritual act of "making" or manifesting something out of nothing through desire.</note> and the Will is a <term>Magus</term><note>The "Magus" is the actor or the directing mind that uses the "Magic" (craving) to create.</note>, and that the Will is greater than its mother who gives it: for he is Lord in the mother, and the mother is recognized as mute, and the Will as a life without <term>origin</term><note>original: *Ursprung*. Böhme often uses terms like *Urstand* or *Ursprung* to describe the "primal state" or "first standing" of a thing.</note>; and yet the craving is a cause of the Will, but without recognition or understanding, and the Will is the understanding of the craving.

3. Thus we give you in brief to contemplate the nature and the spirit of nature, what has been from eternity without <term>primal origin</term><note>original: *Urstand*.</note>, and find thus that the Will, as the spirit, has no place of its rest; but the craving is its own place, and the Will is a bond upon it, and yet is also not grasped by it.

## The Third Text.

### Summaries.

The Will is the Eternal Omnipotence, and rules over the craving, and governs the life of the craving. §. 1. 2. The Eternal Spirit of the Will is GOD. 3. 4.

SO then, as the eternal Will is free from the craving, and yet the craving is not free from the Will—for the Will rules over the craving—so we recognize the Will as the eternal Omnipotence. For it has nothing like itself, and the craving is indeed a movement of pulling or desiring, but without understanding, and has a life, but without knowledge.

2. Now the Will governs the life of the craving, and does with it what he wills: and though he does something, it is nonetheless not recognized until that same essence reveals itself with the Will, so that it becomes an essence in the life of the Will; thus is it recognized what the Will has made.

3. And so we recognize the eternal Will-Spirit as GOD, and the driving life of the craving as Nature. For there is nothing earlier, and both are without beginning, and each is a cause of the other, and an eternal bond.

<vocab>Will, Craving, Magic, Magus, Primal Origin, Nature, Omnipotence, GOD</vocab>

<summary>This page explains the dialectical relationship between the "Will" (identified as the Spirit and God) and "Craving" (identified as the driving force of Nature), where the Will acts as the intelligent master over the unconscious desire of creation.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, The Will, Longing (Sucht), Magic (Magia), Magus, God and Nature, Omnipotence, Mystical Philosophy</keywords>

--- Page 119 ---
<meta>This page concludes the "Third Text" regarding the relationship between the Will and the Unground, and begins the "Fourth Text," which details the process of spiritual "pregnancy" where the Will generates a Word or Sound to manifest itself from nothingness.</meta>

<page-num>99</page-num>
<header>Text 4. and the Heavenly Mystery.</header>

4. And thus the Will-Spirit is an eternal knowledge of the <term>Unground</term><note>original: *Ungrund*. Böhme uses this term to describe the "abyss" or the "groundless" state of absolute nothingness that exists before God or the universe is manifested.</note>, and the life of the <term>longing</term><note>original: *Sucht*. In Böhme’s vocabulary, this is the driving, almost "addictive" urge for existence that pushes the Spirit toward creation.</note> is an eternal essence of the Will.

## The Fourth Text.

### Summaries.

The longing is a desire, §. 1. and the desire is an attraction. 2. The Will takes where there is nothing, and becomes pregnant; 3. gives birth within itself, 4. namely a word or sound, 5. and opens the intelligent life of <term>Magic</term><note>original: *Magiâ*. Böhme defines Magic as the spiritual power through which the Will creates something out of nothing.</note>. 6. The threefold Spirit is its master: the word is its seat, 7. 8. and stands in the middle as a heart. ibid. Thus God and Nature are from eternity. 9.

So, then, as the longing is a desire, and that same desire is a life, so that same desiring life goes forward in the longing, and is always pregnant with the longing.

2. And the desire is a stern attraction, and yet has nothing but itself, as the eternity without ground; now it draws magically, as its desire itself toward a substance.

3. For the Will now takes where there is nothing; it is a lord and possessor; it is itself no essence, and yet rules in the essence; and the essence makes it desiring, namely for the essence. And so as it then becomes desiring within itself, it is magical, and impregnates itself, as with spirit without essence; for in its <term>primal state</term><note>original: *Urstand*. This refers to the original condition or foundation of a being.</note> it is only spirit. Thus it creates in its <term>imagination</term><note>original: *Imagination*. For Böhme, this is the creative faculty of the mind to "image" or give form to spiritual potential.</note> only spirit, and becomes pregnant with the spirit, as the eternal knowledge of the Unground, in the omnipotence of life, without essence.

4. And so when it is then pregnant, the birthing goes into itself, and dwells within itself. For the other life-essence cannot grasp this <term>pregnancy</term><note>original: *Schwängerung*. This refers to the internal process of the Will becoming filled with its own idea or Word.</note> and be its container. Thus the pregnancy must go into itself, and be its own container, as a Son in the eternal Spirit.

5. And because this pregnancy has no essence; so it is a voice or sound, as a word of the spirit, and remains in the primal state of the spirit, for it has otherwise no seat, but only in the primal state of the spirit.

6. And yet there is a Will in this word, which there wills to go out into an essence, and that same Will is the life of the <term>primordial</term><note>original: *urständlich*.</note> Will, which goes out from the pregnancy, as from the

A secret, hidden, or concentrated spiritual power.</note> or a mystery of a whole being. Divine mysteries or hidden spiritual realities.</note>: one in the Spirit-life, and one in the essential life. The Spirit-life is recognized as GOD, and is rightly called so; and the essential life as Nature-life, which would have no understanding if the Spirit or Spirit-life were not desiring. In this desire, the Divine Being—as the eternal Word and Heart of GOD—is always and from eternity born; from which the desiring Will eternally goes forth as its Spirit into the Nature-life, and opens therein the mystery out of the essences and in the essences, so that there are two lives, and also two beings, out of and in a single, eternal, unfathomable <term>origin</term><note>original: *Urstand*. The primal state or original condition of being before time began.</note>.

9. And thus we recognize what GOD and nature are, how they are both from eternity, without any ground or beginning; for it is an ever-eternal beginning: It begins itself always and from eternity to eternity, where there is no number, for it is the <term>Unground</term><note>original: *Ungrund*. The "bottomless" or "groundless" void from which all things arise in Böhme’s theology.</note>.

the creature works. 8. With creation, the <term>Wrath</term><note>original: *Grimm*. The water only restrains their fire, so that there is no consuming there, but rather an eternal being in two mysteries within each other, and yet with the distinction of two principles as two kinds of life.

For Böhme, the *Turba* is the destructive force that breaks down a form or life-hold when it loses its harmony with the divine light.</note>, acting as a dying of the captured life.

8. This refers to the harsh, fiery quality of nature when it is not tempered by love or light.</note> which lies hidden in the Mystery within eternity.

9. And we see here, as eternal nature once moved and stirred itself with the creation of the world, that the Wrath was also stirred and has also revealed itself in creatures: just as one finds many evil animals, as well as herbs and trees, and also worms, such as toads, snakes, and the like. Eternal nature bears a disgust for these, and the malice and poison are honored only in their own essence.

10. And for that reason, eternal nature also seeks the end of malice and wants to leave it; then it falls into the **Turba**, as into death—and yet it is no death, but a spewing out into the Mystery, where malice with its life shall stand apart, as in a darkness. For Nature

## The Sixth Text.

->Summaries.<-

The conflict is within the creature. §. 1. 2. From this originates all power of this world. 3. The Multiplicity seeks the One. 4. Then one Lord shall rule the whole world, 5. where the driver will be sought, 6. in the 6000th year, 7. in the day of the completed creation, 8. namely on the 6th day, after midday. 9.

As we thus reflect and recognize, we now find the conflict of all beings, where one is the disgust of the other and treats the other as an enemy.

2. For every will desires a purity without <term>Turba</term> <note>Latin: "turmoil" or "disturbance." The force that disrupts the harmony of a being's internal nature.</note> in the other being, yet itself has the Turba within, and is also the other’s disgust. Now the power of the greater moves over the smaller and holds it in constraint, unless it flees from it; otherwise, the strong rules over the weak. Thus the weak also runs and seeks the goal of the driver and wants to be free of the constraint; and so the goal is sought by all creatures, which stands hidden in the Mystery.

3. And thus and from this originates all power of this world, that one rules over another; and this was not commanded or ordered by the highest Good in the beginning. Instead, it grew out of the Turba, where nature afterwards recognized it as her own essence—that which was born out of her—and gave it the law to continue giving birth to itself in the established government. Then this birth ascended to the Royal Status, and thereafter sought the abyss as One, until it became a Monarchy, as an Empire; and there it is still ascending and wants to be One, and not Many: and although it is in the Many, the first <term>Source-quality</term> <note>original: *Quall*. A term Böhme uses to describe the "welling up" or internal driving force of a being, often involving a sense of struggle or friction.</note> from which all is born wants to rule over everything, and wants to be the only Lord over all governments.

4. And because this same longing was one government in the beginning, but in time divided into many according to the essences; so the Multiplicity seeks the **ONE** again, and will certainly be born in the sixth number of the crowns, as in the six-thousandth year in the figure: Not at the end, but...

## The Seventh Text.
### Summaries.

One <term>Magic</term> <note>original: *Magia*. Not sorcery, but the formative, creative power of the will that shapes reality.</note> has awakened the other through desire, §. 1. where evil was opened as well, 2. Everything has grown without premeditation, including the colors, 3. such as blue, red, green, yellow; whiteness belongs to GOD. 4. 5. Also we find herein the tree of tongues, with 4 alphabets, in which the Language of Nature <note>original: *Natur-Sprache*. The mystical idea that words once perfectly expressed the inner essence of things.</note> lies, 6. as the first alphabet and the root in all languages; [in the same place] <note>original: *ibid.*</note> afterwards the Hebrew, 7. Greek, 8. and Latin. 9. The opener of all alphabets is God’s Spirit. 10. They originate from the colors of the great Mystery and divide into 77 languages, 11. as can be recognized at the Tower of Babel; 12. where each language entered into itself, in its own reason, 13. from which the Turba has grown. 14. 15.

Since an <term>Arcanum</term> <note>Latin: "secret" or "hidden essence." Here it refers to the deep spiritual potential within the natural world.</note> lies within the Mystery of eternal nature, from which all creatures, both evil and good, have been born and created; thus we recognize it as a magical being, where one Magic has awakened the other through desire and brought it into being, where every thing has [itself]...

...elevated itself and led into the highest power: For the Spirit of God is not a maker within nature, but an opener and seeker of the good.

2. Thus, evil, through a magical craving, has always sought and found itself within the <term>Mystery</term> <note>original: *Mysterio*. The hidden, unmanifest source of all things.</note>, and was revealed without God’s intent: For the <term>Wrath</term> <note>original: *Grimm*. The harsh, energetic force in nature that can become bitter or evil if not tempered by light.</note> is a severity, and it rules over the <term>simple</term> <note>original: *Albere*. Refers to the innocent or primordial state of things before they are shaped by stronger forces.</note>.

3. Thus, everything has grown from its own tree without premeditation: For the first opener, God, did not ordain malice to rule: Instead, reason and wit were meant to reveal the wonders and be a guide for life. And here we encounter the great secret that has lain in the Mystery from eternity: the Mystery and its colors, which are four in number; the fifth color is not characteristic of the Mystery of Nature, but of the Mystery of the Deity, which shines in the Mystery of Nature as a living light.

4. And these are the colors in which everything lies: (1) blue, (2) red, (3) green, and (4) yellow; the fifth, which is White, belongs to God, yet it also has its luster in nature. But it is the <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>original: *Essentia*. The "Quintessence," or the purest, most spiritualized form of matter.</note>, a pure and undefiled child, as can be perceived in gold and silver, as well as in a white, bright crystal-stone that withstands the fire.

5. For the fire is the <term>test</term> <note>original: *Proba*. An alchemical term for a trial or proof by fire.</note> of all colors, in which none can endure except for White, because it is a luster of God’s majesty. (The color black does not belong in the Mystery, but is the cover or the darkness in which everything lies.)

6. Here we also find the tree of tongues—that is, of languages—with 4 alphabets: one marked with the <term>Characters</term> <note>original: *Characteren*. Not just letters, but symbolic imprints of spiritual power.</note> of the Mystery, in which lies the <term>Language of Nature</term> <note>original: *Natur-Sprache*. The mystical concept of a lost, universal language where names perfectly express the inner essence of things.</note>, which is the root of all languages: yet it is not recognized in the offspring of the multiplicity (or the many languages), except by its own children, to whom the Mystery itself gives understanding, for it is a wonder of God. (This alphabet of the Language of Nature lies hidden among all others in the black color; for the color black is not counted among the colors, it is Mystery, and is not understood except by the one who possesses the Language of Nature, to whom it is revealed by the Spirit of God.)

7. And the second alphabet is the Hebrew, which opens the <term>Mystery</term> <note>original: *Mysterium*. The hidden, unmanifest source of all things.</note> and names the tree with its branches and twigs.

8. The third is the Greek, which names the tree with its fruit and all its ornament, and which first truly expresses the <term>wisdom</term> <note>original: *Wiße*. A term denoting the specific "manner," "mode," or "essential knowledge" of a thing’s nature.</note>.

9. The fourth is the Latin, which many peoples and tongues make use of; it expresses the tree with its power and virtue.

10. And the fifth is God’s Spirit, who is the opener of all alphabets; and no human being can learn that same alphabet unless it opens itself within the human spirit.

11. Thus, these alphabets originate from the colors of the great Mystery and further divide themselves into a total of 77 languages; yet we recognize only five as the primary languages, and 72 for the "wonders," in which <term>Babel</term> <note>Refers to the biblical Tower of Babel, symbolizing the confusion of tongues and the loss of unified spiritual understanding.</note> is understood as the mouth of a confused being. This happened when Reason abandoned its leader and wished to walk alone and climb into the Mystery.

12. This is recognizable in the children of Nimrod at the Tower of Babel, when they had fallen from obedience to God into their own reason; thus they lost their leader and confused their reason so that they no longer understood their own language.

13. Thus many languages grew—seventy-two out of the confused Babel—and each one turned into itself and sought its own <term>wisdom</term> <note>original: *Wiße*.</note>, each within its own reason and malice. For they had abandoned God and became heathens, and God let them go into their "wonders," for they did not wish to cling to Him but wanted to be their own "growth," and their own reason (which was nonetheless mixed with all the colors) was meant to rule them.

14. Now the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: *Turba*. In this context, the "Turba" refers to a state of spiritual confusion, disturbance, or the "wrathful" element in nature that disrupts divine harmony.</note> was born, so that they were no longer of one mind; for each one wanted to live out of his own "color," and yet these were not the true primary colors, but only their evil, self-hatched children that they brooded over within their own reason. And they left behind the true leader, who had created everything into One Tongue and opened no more than one: a tree with its branches, power, and fruit.

## The Eighth Text.
### Summaries.

There are two kinds of religions, §. 1. and <term>Babel</term> <note>Referring to the spiritual "confusion" of institutional religion that has lost its connection to the divine source.</note> is in both. 2. One plays the hypocrite to God with the mouth, 3. is the <term>Magician</term> <note>original: *Magus*. Here used to describe one who manipulates the creative power of the will for selfish ends.</note> of multiplicity, a devourer, 4. and does not stand in the free will of God; 5. is a renegade and gives birth to falsehood, 6. from which comes a separation from God. 7. 8. Thus there were two kinds of heathens: (1) those who remained in their magic; 9. and (2) those who lived in the flesh and sought war. 10. 11. Likewise were the Jews, 12. and exactly thus is the birth of the <term>Antichrist</term> <note>In Böhme's terminology, the "Antichrist" is not just a person but a spirit of self-interest and false piety residing within the church.</note>, since two kingdoms dwell together in one people, which cannot be mixed in the inner spirit. 13. He is in all houses; but the worst is the crowned whore and her godparents. 14. The other part, belonging to the free knowledge of God, are the true children of God, 15. and they are free from Babel and the Antichrist. 16.

1. Thus we now see the origin of two kinds of religions, from which Babel was born as an idol, and that among both the heathens and the Jews.

2. For Babel is in both, and there are two generations in one: One, which proceeds from its own reason (as from the life of nature and spirit) and seeks to exalt itself; this carves a path for itself in its own being, for its will proceeds from its own craving and seeks its <term>Magic</term> <note>original: *Magiam*. The shaping power of the soul's will.</note> as a great number for its dominion—a multiplicity—and simply goes out from itself, forward; its will remains in its multiplicity and is the God and leader of its own multiplicity.

3. And although the free will of God opposes him and rebukes him, the idol only plays the hypocrite to the free will—that is, to the Spirit of God—with the mouth, and honors its own will in the number of multiplicity: For that same will is born from its own treasure, as from its magic; it does not comprehend the free will of God, and therefore it is born of flesh and blood, from its own nature, and is a child of this world, and considers its treasure to be its love; thus it is now a hypocrite, and a confused Babel. For

<meta>the</meta>

<summary>Böhme describes the spiritual divide between true divine knowledge and "Babel," the institutionalized confusion where human reason and self-will masquerade as religion. He argues that this internal conflict exists within all peoples, giving rise to the spirit of the Antichrist through hypocrisy and a focus on worldly multiplicity.</summary>

<keywords>Babel, Antichrist, Magia, free will, hypocrisy, reason, religious history, multiplicity, heathens, Jews</keywords>

the numbers of multiplicity, as his own <term>Magic</term> <note>original: *Magia*. In this context, it refers to the human ability to manipulate the world through the will, which becomes corrupted when not aligned with God.</note>, confuse him, so that he goes out from one number into many; now this multiplicity is a confused <term>Babel</term> <note>Symbolizing the confusion of languages and the spiritual "noise" of institutionalized religion.</note>, and his hypocritical mouth, with which he gives the Spirit of Unity good words and praises much, is an <term>Antichrist</term> and a liar: for he speaks one way and acts another; his heart is an addiction <note>original: *Sucht*. This implies a sickly, obsessive craving or a spiritual malady.</note>, and the spirit of his heart has turned itself into that addiction.

4. Thus the Magician of multiplicity is now a proud, haughty, greedy, malicious devourer, and a spirit out of the craving multiplicity, and is a false idolater: he does not cling to the free will of nature—which has the power of wonders in its authority—and has no understanding in the divine <term>Mystery</term> <note>original: *Mysterio*. The hidden spiritual reality underlying all of creation.</note>; for he does not cling to that same Spirit with his will. Otherwise, if his will were turned toward freedom, then the Spirit of God would open His magical mystery, and His wonders and works would stand within his will in God.

5. But since they now go out from themselves, the beginning seeks the end, and the middle is the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: *Turba*. A Latin term used by Jacob Böhme to describe the "awakened wrath" or the spiritual "commotion" that disrupts divine harmony when things move away from God.</note>. For it does not stand in the free will of God, but it grows out of itself and raises itself up as a proud tree.

6. And as God is only one in will, and one in the eternal desire—as in the eternal <term>Magic</term>—so that the craving of the eternal Magic now surrenders itself into the eternal will and draws its life therein, so the will (which originates out of the birth as a renegade) is a perjured whore; for it is a mother of falsehood and does not cling to the free will.

7. And here we understand a separation from God, of which Lucifer is the cause of it all, who has made the Magic of nature falsely addicted: and thus two eternal lives are born in this—one in God’s will, and the other in the Devil’s and the wrath’s will; and that is Babel with the Antichrist on Earth.

8. Everything that goes out from God's will into its own will belongs in Babel; you see this in the Jews and Heathens, as well as in all nations.

9. The Heathens remained standing in their own magic: but those who went out from the addiction of corruption into the light of nature—because they did not know God—and lived in pu-
rity,

[U]nity; these were the children of the free will, and in them the Spirit of Freedom opened great wonders in their Mystery, as can be seen in the wisdom they left behind.

10. The others, however, who lived only in their own magical will, out of flesh and blood, had their will drowned in the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: *Turba*. A Latin term meaning "turmoil" or "disturbance." In this context, it refers to the chaotic, destructive wrath of nature that breaks things apart when they leave the divine order.</note>, and the Turba surged up in their will, and gave them a spirit according to the essences of greed and ferocity; they sought only the number of multiplicity, such as lordships and kingdoms.

11. And when the Turba could not proceed by force, it grew enraged and began strife and war; and from this, war originates—born from the pride and greed of multiplicity; and it belongs with its "number" in the Mystery of Wrath.

12. The Jews were likewise: **God** revealed Himself to them, but they also clung to two wills. One part followed the Commandment, with their will directed into **God's** will, such as the Patriarchs and all pious hopers of Israel. The others performed the work of the Law with their hands, but clung with their will to their poisoned <term>Magic</term> <note>original: *Magia*. This refers to the human capacity to shape reality through the will; when "poisoned," it becomes a tool for selfish or worldly gain rather than divine service.</note>, such as greed, and sought only their numbers of multiplicity. Their mouth was a Jew, but the heart was a Babylonian Whore, a hypocrite and Antichrist, with good words and a false, greedy heart.

13. And thus, within Christendom and among all peoples, the Babylonian Whore has settled in with the Antichrist, where two kingdoms dwell simultaneously within one people. In the inner spirit, they do not allow themselves to be mixed so that they might become one, just as clay and iron do not mix; they certainly mix according to the body, but their spirits are of two different kinds, as the Prophet <margin>Daniel, chap. 2: 43.</margin> Daniel says in chapter 2, verse 43.

14. Therefore, whoever wishes to recognize the Antichrist, let him only seek him thus: he will find him in every house. But the worst is the "crowned whore"; and her godparents, who raise her from the "baptism of whoredom" (so that they too may live in the number of multiplicity), are the loud-mouthed preachers who lead people away from the single will of **God** into many conflicting wills, so that they may only inherit the number of multiplicity and fatten their earthly bellies.

<meta>catchword: 15. And</meta>

It emphasizes that true spirituality is found in the "Unity" of God's will rather than the "multiplicity" of worldly power and institutional hypocrisy.</summary>

15. For the author, "Magic" is the spiritual power of the will to shape reality and connect with the divine.</note>. For **God's** Spirit dwells in their will and reveals to them the eternal wonders of **God**, and their life-spirit reveals the wonders of this world.

16. And these are free from <term>Babylon</term> <note>original: *Babel*. Symbolizes the "confusion" of false, institutionalized religion and worldly pride.</note> and the Antichrist, even if they were sitting in its very lap. For the true image of **God** stands in the will-spirit, which is born from the soul-spirit.

## The Ninth Text.
### Summaries.

As there are two Magics, so too are there two spirits that lead them. §. 1. One must be in earnest to tame the star-spirit, for it is not such an easy thing to become a child of **God**. 2. For which the Antichrist falsely passes himself off. 3. Therefore the world may well examine itself in these writings. 4. 5. For Babylon is already catching fire, and her kingdom draws to an end. Hallelujah! 6.

So then, as two Magics are within one another, so also are there two Mages <note>original: *Magi*. Spiritual practitioners or those who direct their will.</note> who lead them, like two spirits. One is **God's** Spirit, and the other is the spirit of Reason, into which the devil weaves himself; and in **God's** Spirit is the love of unity. And a person can test themselves no better than by noticing in earnest what their desire and pleasure drive them toward; whichever one drives them, that one they have as a leader, and they are also that leader's child. Yet they still have power now to break and change that same will, for the person is magical and possesses the power of the will.

2. But it must be done in earnest: for one must tame the star-spirit <note>original: *Sternen-Geist*. The "sidereal" or astral influence that dictates human temperament, moods, and worldly desires based on the physical heavens.</note> that rules within; to this belongs a sober, quiet life, with constant casting of oneself into **God's** will. For to tame the <term>star-torment</term> <note>original: *Sternen-Qual*. The restless, driving energy or "anxiety" of the physical cosmos as it operates within the human soul.</note>, no wisdom or art is sufficient; rather, it requires temperance of life, with a constant withdrawal from the [astral] influences. The elements always throw the star-craving into the will. Therefore it is not such an easy...

-> <vocab>Magic, Antichrist, Babylon, Star-spirit, Reason-spirit, Soul-spirit, Mages, Elements, Magic</vocab> <-

...no easy thing to become a child of **God**; it requires great labor with much toil and suffering.

3. And yet the Antichrist dares to call himself a child of **God**; but Christ says: Not everyone who says to me, "Lord, Lord," shall enter the kingdom of heaven; have we not cast out demons in your name and done [mighty] deeds? But He says to them: Depart from me, you stinking goats <note>A reference to the biblical separation of the sheep (the saved) and the goats (the damned), used here to emphasize moral corruption.</note>, I do not know you. Matthew 7:22. You have done this out of false <term>Magic</term> <note>original: *Magia*. Here meaning the use of spiritual or mental power driven by the selfish ego rather than divine love.</note>, and were never recognized in my Spirit and Will. In your spiritual character, you are goats, tyrants, greedy misers, the proud, and the sensual; you have carried my name on your tongues, but sacrificed your heart to sensuality and the lusts of the flesh, and were born in the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: *Turba*. A Latin term for "turmoil" or "disturbance." In this philosophy, it refers to the destructive, chaotic wrath of nature that breaks things apart when they are not aligned with God's light.</note>. You must be tested through the fire, so that each kingdom may receive its own fruit.

4. Therefore, you beautiful world, examine yourself in these writings which the eternal Foundation <note>original: *Grund*. Refers to the *Ungrund* or the primal source of all being.</note> has set before you, and contemplate them more deeply and further, or you will be snatched up in your <term>Turba</term>; there you shall go with your being through the fire of **God**, and whatever work exists outside of **God's** will shall remain in the fire.

5. But what is born in **God's** will shall stand to **God's** honor and wonder-work, and for the eternal joy of the human image.

6. Now consider what you do! For <term>Babylon</term> <note>original: *Babel*. Represents the "confusion" of hypocritical, institutional religion and the pursuit of worldly power.</note> is already in flames and burning; there is no more quenching it, nor any medicine: she has been recognized as evil, and her kingdom comes to an end. Hallelujah!
